- **aeja-literature-positioning**: Use when the marginal contribution of an American Economic Journal: Applied Economics (AEJ: Applied) manuscript is fuzzy or undersold and must be staked precisely against prior applied-micro work. - **aeja-referee-strategy**: Use when anticipating the objections a sophisticated American Economic Journal: Applied Economics (AEJ: Applied) referee will raise, so a manuscript pre-empts them before submission or addresses them in revision. - **aeja-replication-package**: Use when assembling the data and code replication package for an American Economic Journal: Applied Economics (AEJ: Applied) manuscript to pass the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy and the AEA Data Editor's pre-publication reproducibility check. - **aeja-robustness**: Use when an American Economic Journal: Applied Economics (AEJ: Applied) manuscript's headline estimate must be shown to survive specification, sample, and inference choices before submission or in an R&R. - **aeja-tables-figures**: Use when building or revising the exhibits of an American Economic Journal: Applied Economics (AEJ: Applied) manuscript so the main causal result is legible in one table or figure and respects AEA house presentation norms. - **aeja-writing-style**: Use when revising the prose, abstract, and introduction of an American Economic Journal: Applied Economics (AEJ: Applied) manuscript so the design and headline estimate land in the first paragraph in AEA house style. Polishes exposition; it does not change the result (aeja-identification / aeja-robustness) or the exhibits. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Applied-Economics-Skills/skills/aeja-writing-style) · ★1.1k)
- **aejmac-identification**: Use when the empirical identification of a macro shock or dynamic causal effect is the bottleneck for an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript — SVAR, local projections, narrative, high-frequency/proxy-VAR, or micro-data macro designs. - **aejmac-literature-positioning**: Use when the contribution of an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript relative to the macro frontier is fuzzy, contested, or undersold. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Macroeconomics-Skills/skills/aejmac-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **aejmac-rebuttal**: Use when an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) decision letter has arrived (R&R or reject-with-comments) and you need a response-letter strategy and revision plan. - **aejmac-referee-strategy**: Use when anticipating the objections referees and coeditors will raise on an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript, and calibrating desk-reject and revision odds, before submission. - **aejmac-replication-package**: Use when assembling the data, code, and documentation package for an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript to pass the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy and the AEA Data Editor's pre-publication reproducibility check. - **aejmac-robustness**: Use when the headline result of an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript must be shown stable across specification, sample, identification, and tuning choices. - **aejmac-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for the American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) via the AEA submission system — membership-scaled fee, JEL codes, PDF and length rules, ≤100-word abstract, disclosure statements, and data/code readiness. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Macroeconomics-Skills/skills/aejmac-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **aejmac-tables-figures**: Use when building or revising exhibits for an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript — impulse-response figures, fan charts, model-fit overlays, and regression/moment tables — to AEA house standards and macro conventions. Formatting and clarity; it does not generate the underlying estimates. - **aejmac-theory-model**: Use when the quantitative model is the bottleneck for an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript — DSGE, New Keynesian, heterogeneous-agent (HANK / Aiyagari-Bewley), or structural estimation — and calibration, parameter identification, solution accuracy, or counterfactual validity need discipline. - **aejmac-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a macro paper fits the American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) rather than AER, J. Monetary Economics, RED, or AEJ: Applied. - **aejmac-workflow**: Use when deciding which aejmac-* sub-skill to invoke next, or when sequencing manuscript work from topic selection through rebuttal for an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) submission. - **aejmac-writing-style**: Use when revising prose, the abstract, or the introduction of an American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics (AEJ: Macro) manuscript for AEA house style and the broad-interest macro arc. - **aejmic-identification**: Use when the question is what makes the result tight or what the data identify for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript — covering both (a) structural/empirical-IO and experimental identification and (b) for pure theory, which assumptions drive the result and how robust the mechanism is. - **aejmic-literature-positioning**: Use when the contribution of an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript is fuzzy or oversold relative to the closest existing results. - **aejmic-rebuttal**: Use when drafting the response letter and revision plan after an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) R&R. - **aejmic-referee-strategy**: Use when anticipating referee objections or calibrating review expectations for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript before submission. - **aejmic-replication-package**: Use when assembling the proof appendix and any code/data deposit for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript under the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy. - **aejmic-robustness**: Use when extensions, edge cases, or applied robustness checks are missing for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript — covering theory extensions (relaxed assumptions, alternative concepts, perturbations) and applied/experimental robustness. Decides which extensions earn their place; it does not prove the main result (see aejmic-theory-model). - **aejmic-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for the American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) via the AEA submission system — single-blind review, the 100-word abstract, JEL classification, the submission fee, format, and the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy. - **aejmic-tables-figures**: Use when presenting results for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript — propositions, numerical examples, schematic theory figures, and empirical/experimental tables. - **aejmic-theory-model**: Use when the model setup, equilibrium concept, or proof architecture is the bottleneck for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript — the central skill for a theory-first paper. - **aejmic-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a microeconomics project fits the American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) rather than JET, GEB, Theoretical Economics, Econometrica, or AEJ: Applied. - **aejmic-workflow**: Use when deciding which aejmic-* sub-skill to invoke next, or when sequencing manuscript work from topic selection through rebuttal for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) submission. Routes — it does not replace — the specialized skills. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Microeconomics-Skills/skills/aejmic-workflow) · ★1.1k)
- **aejmic-writing-style**: Use when prose buries the result or the abstract/intro do not land for an American Economic Journal: Microeconomics (AEJ: Micro) manuscript. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Microeconomics-Skills/skills/aejmic-writing-style) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-identification**: Use when the credibility of the causal evaluation of a policy is the bottleneck for an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript — DID/event study, IV, RDD/bunching, or RCT of a program. - **aejpol-literature-positioning**: Use when placing the contribution against the policy-evaluation literature and sharpening it as a policy answer for an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-rebuttal**: Use when an AEJ: Economic Policy decision letter (R&R or reject-and-resubmit) has arrived and a response-letter strategy and revision plan are needed. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-referee-strategy**: Use when anticipating how AEJ: Economic Policy referees and editors will read a manuscript, before submission or before an R&R, to pre-empt the objections that sink policy-evaluation papers. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-referee-strategy) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-replication-package**: Use when assembling the data and code deposit for an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript to satisfy the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy and the AEA Data Editor's pre-publication reproducibility check. Builds the openICPSR deposit, README, and restricted-data access paths; it does not run the analysis or write the paper. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-replication-package) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-robustness**: Use when an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript's headline policy estimate needs to be shown stable and credible against specification, sample, inference, and identification threats. Organizes the robustness program by threat-to-the-policy-conclusion; it does not design the primary identification or write exhibits. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-robustness) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for AEJ: Economic Policy via the AEA online submission system — single-blind review front matter, JEL codes, submission and publication fees, format, the Data and Code Availability Statement, and disclosure. Final checks; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-tables-figures**: Use when building or revising exhibits for an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript so they meet AEA house style and carry the policy message — no significance asterisks, a self-contained headline exhibit, and figures that show the policy effect with uncertainty. Designs exhibits; it does not run the estimation or write the surrounding prose. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-tables-figures) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-theory-model**: Use when an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript needs a framework that maps reduced-form estimates into a welfare, cost-benefit, or distributional policy object — sufficient statistics, MVPF, optimal-policy, or a small applied model. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-theory-model) · ★1.1k)
- **aejpol-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a project fits AEJ: Economic Policy rather than J. Public Economics, AEJ: Applied, or AER, and when sharpening the policy question and its welfare stake for an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript. Frames fit and the policy-question-first contribution; it does not design the identification or run estimation. - **aejpol-workflow**: Use when deciding which aejpol-* sub-skill to invoke next, or when sequencing manuscript work from topic selection through rebuttal for an AEJ: Economic Policy manuscript. Routes — it does not replace — the specialized skills. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AEJ-Economic-Policy-Skills/skills/aejpol-workflow) · ★1.1k)

([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/project-setup/e2e-skill-scaffolding) · ★18)
- **aep-easy-explain**: Stop. That last message did not land — re-pitch it. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/patterns/easy-explain) · ★18)
- **aep-envision**: Validates and frames a product opportunity into the Context Document downstream agents read. Use to start or revisit an idea, or for "what should we build". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/product-context/envision) · ★18)
- **aep-executor**: Spawns and steers workspace agents across Claude Code and Codex backends. Use for backend choice, launch mode, or host detection; other skills call it. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/patterns/executor) · ★18)
- **aep-framework**: Agile Engineering Process (Align-Execute-Prove) methodology with phase planning, test matrices, and evaluation harnesses ([mattmre/EVOKORE-MCP-PUBLIC](https://github.com/mattmre/EVOKORE-MCP-PUBLIC/tree/HEAD/SKILLS/ORCHESTRATION%20FRAMEWORK/aep-framework) · ★3)
- **aep-gen-eval**: Defines the reusable generator/evaluator pattern: scoring, agent contracts, eval protocol. Validating one specific artifact is /aep-validate. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/patterns/gen-eval) · ★18)
- **aep-git-ref**: Canonical AEP git reference: base resolution, worktree lifecycle, naming, task commits, PRs, recovery. Use when a git convention is needed. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/agentic-development-workflow/git-ref) · ★18)
- **aep-handoff**: Carry the work into a fresh session. Use when this session is ending or has run long, and the next session must pick the work up. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-handoff) · ★1)
- **aep-help**: What AEP is and what to reach for. Use when the question is about AEP itself — what to reach for, and when. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-help) · ★1)
- **aep-human-alignment**: Renders product-context.yaml into where we stand, what waits on a human, and what drifted. Use for pulse, status check, or a project brief; not planning. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/human-alignment) · ★18)
- **aep-implement**: Carry the effort to a finished stack. Use when a task exists and is ready to build. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-implement) · ★1)
- **aep-install**: Join a repository to AEP. Use when a repository has no .aep/ directory and should start running AEP. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-install) · ★1)
- **aep-launch**: Creates a designed feature's worktree and spawns its implementation agent, plus an optional evaluator. Use after /aep-design or for "launch workspace". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/agentic-development-workflow/launch) · ★18)
- **aep-map**: Decomposes a product into a system map, layered story graph, and agent topology. Use after /aep-envision, or for "break this down" and "plan the stories". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/product-context/map) · ★18)
- **aep-model**: Builds a human-reviewed OOUX object map and noun-first UI blueprint. Use after /aep-map, before UI stories, or for "object model" and "OOUX". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/product-context/model) · ★18)
- **aep-onboard**: Installs, verifies, and explains AEP itself: environment, tools, plugin config. Use for first contact with AEP; creating a project is /aep-scaffold. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/project-setup/onboard) · ★18)
- **aep-plan**: Define HOW it will be built. Use when a spec is settled and the technical approach is not yet decided. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-plan) · ★1)
- **aep-prose**: Make it read as though a person wrote it. Use when about to emit text a human will read, or editing text that reads as though nobody wrote it. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-prose) · ★1)
- **aep-prototype**: Answer it by building. Use when a technical or design question will not settle on paper and needs building to answer. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-prototype) · ★1)
- **aep-prune**: Remove what is no longer true. Use when the .aep/ tree has accumulated stale, contradicted, or orphaned artifacts. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-prune) · ★1)
- **aep-reflect**: Classifies post-ship feedback and routes it to the story graph, architecture, or hypothesis. Use after /aep-wrap or user testing, or for "what did we learn". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/product-context/reflect) · ★18)
- **aep-scaffold**: Creates a new AEP project or repairs an existing project's agentic infrastructure and skills layout. Installing AEP itself is /aep-onboard. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/project-setup/scaffold) · ★18)
- **aep-specify**: Define WHAT is changing and WHY. Use when a change is wanted and no effort describes it yet. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-specify) · ★1)
- **aep-survey**: Find where the architecture is costing you. Use when the question is where the codebase is costing you, rather than a specific change. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-survey) · ★1)
- **aep-tasks**: Derive executable work from the spec. Use when a spec is accepted and needs to become executable work. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-tasks) · ★1)
- **aep-tdd**: Test-driven development. Use when building a behaviour test-first, or pinning a bug down before fixing it. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-tdd) · ★1)
- **aep-update**: Move a repository to the running release. Use when the running AEP release differs from the one this repository declares, protocol files look wrong, or the repository still carries a 1.x or 2.x layout. ([saud-alnasser/skills](https://github.com/saud-alnasser/skills/tree/HEAD/src/adapters/opencode/skills/aep-update) · ★1)
- **aep-validate**: Validates one artifact — code, document, or spec — with separate generator and evaluator roles. Use for code review, "validate", "verify", or "dry-run". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/product-context/validate) · ★18)
- **aep-watch**: Ingests bug trackers, errors, and telemetry, then dedupes them into backlog stories. Use for "monitor for new work" or telemetry-driven stories. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/product-context/watch) · ★18)
- **aep-workflow**: Authors a re-runnable multi-agent harness for one task too large or uncertain for a single context. Use for "dynamic workflow" or "orchestrate subagents". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/patterns/workflow) · ★18)
- **aep-workflow-feedback**: Captures process learnings in a downstream project and routes them upstream to AEP. Use after /aep-wrap or /aep-reflect, or for "capture learnings". ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/patterns/workflow-feedback) · ★18)
- **aep-wrap**: Archives OpenSpec artifacts and removes the workspace after a PR merges. Use for "wrap up", "archive", or post-merge cleanup. ([memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns](https://github.com/memorysaver/agentic-engineering-patterns/tree/HEAD/skills/agentic-development-workflow/wrap) · ★18)
- **aequitas**: Für Aequitas: ordnet Norm, Beweislast und Gegenargument; Ergebnis: Prüfprodukt mit Risiko und nächstem Schritt. ([Klotzkette/claude-fuer-deutsches-recht](https://github.com/Klotzkette/claude-fuer-deutsches-recht/tree/HEAD/roemisches-recht/skills/aequitas) · ★1.6k)
- **aer-consistency**: Use when auditing a finished or near-finished AER, AER:Insights, or AEJ manuscript for internal consistency: headline numbers across abstract, introduction, results, and tables; sample sizes; log-point and percentage-point conversions; cross-references; and in-text-citation/bibliography matching. Apply after the body and exhibits exist, before aer-referee-sim and aer-submission. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-consistency) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-figures**: Use when the user has an R/ggplot2 figure script and wants it formatted for AER or economics journal submission, mentions publication-ready figures, or asks to make a figure journal-compliant ([halidaee/econtools_marketplace](https://github.com/halidaee/econtools_marketplace/tree/HEAD/manuscript-tools/skills/aer-figures) · ★1)
- **aer-identification**: Use when selecting, implementing, or stress-testing the causal identification strategy for an empirical economics manuscript — difference-in-differences (including staggered designs), instrumental variables (including weak-IV-robust inference), regression discontinuity, synthetic control, or shift-share / Bartik. Apply before writing the introduction or results. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-identification) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-insights**: Use when targeting AER: Insights or deciding whether a short-format economics manuscript fits this venue. Encodes the journal's fit, framing, method-and-evidence bar, house style, official-submission re-check, and desk-reject heuristics. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/English-SocialScience-Journal-Skills/skills/aer-insights) · ★1.1k)
- **aer-introduction**: Use when drafting or rewriting the introduction of an economics manuscript targeted at AER, AER:Insights, or an AEJ, or when compressing an abstract to the mandatory 100-word limit. Implements the Keith Head / Bellemare five-paragraph formula and AER-specific formatting conventions. Apply after the body sections exist. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-introduction) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-literature**: Use when positioning a manuscript against the existing economics literature, building the antecedents map for the introduction, deciding what to cite, or verifying that every reference in the bibliography is real, correctly attributed, and cited to the published version. Apply at topic selection for the novelty scan and again before drafting the introduction. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-literature) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-paper-body**: Use when drafting or revising the body sections of an AER, AER:Insights, or AEJ manuscript — institutional background, data, empirical strategy, results, mechanisms, and conclusion. Covers equation conventions, results-paragraph narration, magnitude interpretation, and back-of-envelope policy calculations. Apply after the empirics are stable and before or alongside aer-introduction. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-paper-body) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-preregistration**: Use when the project collects primary data or runs a field, lab, or survey experiment, before the intervention begins — write the pre-analysis plan, size the sample from a power calculation, and register with the AEA RCT Registry. Apply after the design is chosen in aer-identification and before any outcome data are seen. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-preregistration) · ★3.9k · 2 copies)
- **aer-rebuttal**: Use when responding to a Revise & Resubmit decision from AER, AER:Insights, or an AEJ, and a point-by-point response letter plus aligned manuscript revisions are needed. Handles triage, the concede / clarify / push-back decision, and the response-letter format that editors actually read. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-rebuttal) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-referee-sim**: Use when a complete draft exists and needs an adversarial internal review before submission — simulating the AER desk screen and three referee reports with calibrated severity, scoring the paper against the editorial rubric, and producing a prioritized revise list. Apply after aer-consistency passes and before aer-submission; rerun until the simulated verdict is at least major R&R. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-referee-sim) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-replication**: Use when assembling the AEA Data and Code Availability deposit for an AER, AER:Insights, or AEJ acceptance, writing the README, or auditing a replication package before the AEA Data Editor review. Implements the current AEA policy, including the February 2026 Data and Code Availability Policy. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-replication) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-robustness**: Use when the main empirical results exist but the manuscript lacks the robustness, heterogeneity, mechanism, and placebo checks that AER referees will demand. Apply after aer-identification and before aer-introduction so that the value-added paragraph can reference these tests. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-robustness) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-statspai**: Use when aer-identification has fixed the design, after methodology choice and before aer-robustness or aer-tables-figures, to run an AER-track analysis with StatsPAI — the agent-native Python engine and MCP server for causal inference, robustness, sensitivity, and publication-ready table export. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-statspai) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission audit for an AER, AER:Insights, or AEJ manuscript — length, format, cover letter, per-author disclosure statements, file packaging, and routing among the AEA journal family. Apply immediately before clicking submit. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-submission) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-tables**: Use when the user has an R script producing a table and wants it formatted for AER or economics journal submission, mentions AER style, booktabs formatting, journal-ready tables, or asks to make a table publication-ready ([halidaee/econtools_marketplace](https://github.com/halidaee/econtools_marketplace/tree/HEAD/manuscript-tools/skills/aer-tables) · ★1)
- **aer-tables-figures**: Use when constructing or revising regression tables, descriptive statistics tables, or figures after results are estimated and before submission for an AER, AER:Insights, or AEJ manuscript. Implements AER booktabs house style, regression-table layout, and figure-note conventions. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-tables-figures) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-test**: This skill should be used when the user wants to write or run Apex unit tests with aer (the Apex Execution Runtime) — "run aer tests", "run my Apex tests", "run a single test method", "write an Apex test", "debug a failing Apex test", "show System.debug output", "check Apex code coverage", or "find a flaky Apex test". Covers the `aer test` command and its selection / configuration / output / debugging flags, `@IsTest` authoring conventions, and how to read PASS/FAIL output. Do NOT use for deploying metadata, running the `aer server` API, or executing anonymous Apex (`aer exec`). ([octoberswimmer/agent-skills](https://github.com/octoberswimmer/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/aer/skills/aer-test) · ★1)
- **aer-topic-selection**: Use when evaluating whether a research idea clears the AER top-5 bar, when routing between AER, AER:Insights, and the AEJ family, or when sharpening a fuzzy contribution sentence into one publishable claim. Apply before any writing begins. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-topic-selection) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aer-workflow**: Use when deciding which AER-skills sub-skill to use next, before choosing a specialized skill or after a user asks how to sequence manuscript work from topic selection through rebuttal for AER, AER:Insights, or AEJ journals. Routes — does not replace — the specialized skills. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/50-brycewang-aer-skills/skills/aer-workflow) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **aeri-identification**: Use when the identification argument is the bottleneck for an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript — causal identification in an empirical design, parameter identification in a structural model, or treatment-effect identification in an experiment. Stress-tests the strategy so it is clean enough to defend in a few pages, before exhibits are finalized. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-identification) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-literature-positioning**: Use when positioning the contribution of an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript against the closest published work in one or two tight paragraphs. Stakes the gap concisely; it does not write the full literature review (there is no room for one). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-rebuttal**: Use when responding to an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) conditional-accept decision — addressing the editor's letter within the short window, usually without a second referee round. Drafts a concise, decisive response and revision plan; it does not redo the analysis (see aeri-robustness / aeri-identification). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-referee-strategy**: Use when calibrating expectations for the fast, decisive American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) review process — conditional-accept-or-reject decisions, the single-round model, and pre-empting the objections a short paper cannot afford. Explains the process and stress-tests the paper; it does not write the response letter (see aeri-rebuttal). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-referee-strategy) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-replication-package**: Use when assembling the data and code deposit for an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript to pass the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy and the AEA Data Editor's pre-publication reproducibility check. Builds the package and README; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-replication-package) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-robustness**: Use when deciding which robustness checks belong in the few in-text words of an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript versus the online Supplemental Appendix. Triages checks against the length cap; it does not design the identification (see aeri-identification). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-robustness) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) via the AEA/ScholarOne submission system — the word-count and exhibit-count gates, abstract limit, fees, author metadata, declarations, and the AEA Data and Code Availability Policy. Final checks; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-tables-figures**: Use when building or trimming the exhibits of an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript to the hard five-exhibit budget, one page each, no significance asterisks. Designs the minimum exhibit set; it does not run the analysis behind them. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-tables-figures) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-theory-model**: Use when a model — a full theory paper's argument or the structural/conceptual scaffolding behind an empirical AER: Insights short-format manuscript — needs to be made minimal, so it carries the single insight in a few pages. Keeps only what the insight requires; it does not develop a general framework. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-theory-model) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a project is the ONE crisp, important, well-identified insight that an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript requires, rather than a multi-result paper for AER or a field paper for the AEJ family. Tests single-insight fit; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-workflow**: Use when deciding which aeri-* sub-skill to invoke next, or when sequencing work on a short-format manuscript from topic selection through the conditional-accept response for an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) submission. Routes — it does not replace — the specialized skills. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-workflow) · ★1.1k)
- **aeri-writing-style**: Use when revising an American Economic Review: Insights (AER: Insights) short-format manuscript to lead with the result, fit the hard word cap (7,000 words minus 200 per exhibit), and write the ≤100-word abstract. Enforces the length budget and the lead-with-the-result arc; it does not change the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/AER-Insights-Skills/skills/aeri-writing-style) · ★1.1k)
- **aerj-data-analysis**: Use when planning or reporting the analysis for an American Educational Research Journal (AERJ) manuscript — multilevel/HLM and growth models, IRT/measurement, quasi-experimental estimation, or qualitative coding and thematic analysis. - **aerj-literature-positioning**: Use when positioning an American Educational Research Journal (AERJ) manuscript in the literature. Builds the positioning argument; it does not write the literature review for you. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Educational-Research-Journal-Skills/skills/aerj-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **aerj-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response to an American Educational Research Journal (AERJ) revise-and-resubmit. Structures the response letter; it does not fabricate new results. - **aes-related-work**: 在为《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 稿件撰写相关工作与文献综述时调用。覆盖如何界定领域范围、系统检索中外文献(CNKI/万方/IEEE/dblp)、按技术脉络而非流水账组织综述、突出与本文的区别与本文创新点、恰当引用本刊及英文姊妹刊 Chinese Journal of Electronics 的相关论文、GB/T 7714 引用规范、以及双盲阶段自引匿名化。帮助电子/信息/信号处理方向作者写出既显示学科视野、又清晰定位本文贡献的相关工作章节，避免因综述薄弱或漏引关键文献被外审质疑创新性。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-related-work) · ★1.1k)
- **aes-reproducibility**: 当你为《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 稿件强化可复现性与实验可重复时使用。覆盖电子/信息/信号处理/通信类工作的实验环境记录、随机性控制、数据与预处理透明、算法伪代码与超参数完整披露、硬件与仿真工具链版本、统计显著性与多次运行、以及在中文学报正文与附录里如何组织可复现信息。帮助作者用可复现性作为通过《电子学报》外审、抵御"结果无法重复"质疑的核心竞争力，而非事后补救。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-reproducibility) · ★1.1k)
- **aes-review-process**: 当你需要理解《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 审稿流程、稿件状态与时限时使用。覆盖投稿受理→编辑初审→双盲外审(同行评议)→复审→主编/编委会终审→退修与再审轮次→录用的多级审理链路，各阶段可能的决定(录用/退修/退稿)、6 个月未答复红线、如何解读稿件状态、如何礼貌催稿、以及本刊与英文姊妹刊 Chinese Journal of Electronics 的分流关系。帮助电子/信息/通信方向作者对《电子学报》的审理节奏与决策点建立准确预期，合理规划投稿与修回。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-review-process) · ★1.1k)
- **aes-submission**: 在向《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 正式提交稿件前做投稿就绪审计时调用。覆盖官网 ejournal.org.cn 在线投稿系统、注册与稿件同步、官方 Word/LaTeX 模板、中英文摘要与关键词、中图分类号(TN 为主)、GB/T 7714 参考文献、双盲评审的匿名化要求、稿件评审费(中文约 199 元/英文约 260 元 待核实以系统为准)、涉密脱敏、栏目归属与稿件类型选择，并在上传前做退稿风险分诊。适用于把一份电子/信息/通信/信号处理方向的中文原创论文打磨到符合本刊四级审理门槛的场景。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **aes-supplementary**: 当你为《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 稿件组织附录与补充材料时使用。覆盖哪些内容应放正文、哪些应下沉到附录/在线补充材料、多媒体附件(音视频/信号波形/大数据表)的提交方式、公式推导与完整证明的呈现、扩展实验与超参数表、双盲阶段补充材料的匿名化、以及与正文的引用对应关系。帮助电子/信息/信号处理方向作者在《电子学报》有限正文篇幅内保持主线清晰，同时用补充材料完整承载可复现细节与扩展证据。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-supplementary) · ★1.1k)
- **aes-topic-selection**: 当你为《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 判断选题是否对口、定位到合适栏目/学科方向时使用。覆盖本刊电子科学与技术、信息与通信工程、信号与信息处理、电路与系统、电磁场与微波、集成电路等学科侧重，如何评估选题的创新性与本刊契合度、区别于计算机类与控制类兄弟刊、判断投中文《电子学报》还是英文姊妹刊 Chinese Journal of Electronics、以及原创论文与综述的定位差异。帮助作者在动笔前就把选题放进《电子学报》的正确坐标，避免因不对口在初审被退。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **aes-workflow**: 当你需要《电子学报》(Acta Electronica Sinica, AES) 投稿全流程总览与阶段调度时使用。把选题、写作、投稿、双盲外审、多级审理、修回、录用、校样到出版串成一条端到端时间线，指明每个阶段该调用哪个 aes-* 技能、关键决策点、6 个月答复红线、与英文姊妹刊 Chinese Journal of Electronics 的分流时机、以及常见时间陷阱。适合电子/信息/通信方向作者在动笔前或投稿中做整体规划，避免顺序错乱、错过时限或在错误阶段做错误的事。 ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Acta-Electronica-Sinica-Skills/skills/aes-workflow) · ★1.1k)

The principle: good design you act through without noticing; bad design makes you fight it. Use when designing or reviewing a public interface, library, SDK, or command surface. Triggers on "API ergonomics", "is this easy to misuse", "footgun", "pit of success", "review this interface", "review this CLI", "ergonomics". ([markacianfrani/armor](https://github.com/markacianfrani/armor/tree/HEAD/skills/affordance-audit) · ★6)
- **affordance-false-and-anti**: Use this skill when designing or fixing controls that mislead users — elements that look interactive but aren't (false affordance), or controls that need to clearly communicate "you can't do that here" (anti-affordance, like disabled states). Trigger when reviewing UIs where users tap inert elements expecting response, when designing disabled states, when picking treatments for read-only fields, or when a "decorative" element is being mistaken for a button. Sub-aspect of `affordance`; read that first. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/interaction-and-control-principles/skills/affordance-false-and-anti) · ★8)
- **affordance-signifiers**: Use this skill when the design has interactive capability but users aren't *seeing* it — when the affordance is technically present but the signifier (the visible cue that tells users "you can do this") is missing or weak. Trigger when reviewing flat-design UIs where everything looks the same, when designing custom controls, when stakeholders ask "should this be more obvious?", or when picking signifier strength for primary vs. secondary actions. Sub-aspect of `affordance`; read that first.
